# Proposed OEIS comments: A264651 to A275086

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A264651

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264651>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [6557](../papers/06501-07000/06557-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) +36*a(n-3) -18*a(n-4) +186*a(n-5) +83*a(n-6) +144*a(n-7) +684*a(n-8) -1872*a(n-9) +508*a(n-10) -5048*a(n-11) -3240*a(n-12) +720*a(n-13) -95040*a(n-14) +3072*a(n-15) +3456*a(n-16) +13824*a(n-18) for n>21

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 048 576 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 21. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264655>

Paper: [3377](../papers/03001-03500/03377-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence for column k = 1 is true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264670>

Paper: [649](../papers/00501-01000/00649-PROOF.pdf)

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-3) + 3*a(n-4) + 2*a(n-5) - a(n-6) + 4*a(n-7) - a(n-8) - a(n-10).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 4 096 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A265922

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A265922>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [8408](../papers/08001-08500/08408-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) +110*a(n-2) +186*a(n-3) -1189*a(n-4) -2127*a(n-5) +4920*a(n-6) +5776*a(n-7) -9216*a(n-8)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 11 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 47 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 33. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267867

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267867>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3875](../papers/03501-04000/03875-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 21 2016 and Apr 20 2019: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 4 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268554

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268554>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [9893](../papers/09501-10000/09893-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n^3*(2*n-1)*a(n) -6*(4*n-1)*(3*n-1)*(3*n-2)*(4*n-3)*a(n-1)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 10 2016
> For integer r and positive integer s, define sequences {u(n) : n >= 0} and {v(n) : n >= 0} by setting u(n) = [x^(s*n)] F(x)^(r*n) and v(n) = [x^(s*n)] E(x)^(r*n). We conjecture that both u(n) and v(n) satisfy the above supercongruences. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268788

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268788>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10220](../papers/10001-10500/10220-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268892

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268892>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10221](../papers/10001-10500/10221-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A269001

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A269001>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10222](../papers/10001-10500/10222-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A269088

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A269088>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10223](../papers/10001-10500/10223-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```

```

## A270086

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A270086>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3667](../papers/03501-04000/03667-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Mar 11 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 24 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A270257

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A270257>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [988](../papers/00501-01000/00988-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) -2*a(n-3) +a(n-4)
> Empirical for n mod 2 = 0: a(n) = 3*n^2
> Empirical for n mod 2 = 1: a(n) = 3*n^2 - 3

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true, and the closed form behind it is exact for every n. The shape of the triangle is fixed and it is the alphabet 0..n that grows, so this is not a walk count and no transfer matrix applies. Sum over the sets of adjacent pairs instead: a set of pairs forces the values along each of its connected components to alternate, so a bipartite component has n+1 choices (n for the target n+1) and a component with an odd cycle forces 2x = t, which is possible only for one parity of n. Inclusion-exclusion then gives a(n) = A(n) + (-1)^n C(n) with A and C explicit polynomials, and the recurrence above is exactly (E-1)^(deg A+1) (E+1)^(deg C+1) applied to that, so it holds for every n. The two n mod 2 formulas on this entry are the same statement read at each parity, and are exact as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A270934

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A270934>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3668](../papers/03501-04000/03668-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Mar 26 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 24 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A273405

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A273405>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3672](../papers/03501-04000/03672-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, May 22 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 24 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A274723

Paper: [2861](../papers/02501-03000/02861-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical g.f.: x*(5 + 9*x - 24*x^2 - 45*x^3 - 21*x^4 - 4*x^5 + 8*x^6) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 4*x)*(1 + x + x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 30 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ```
